\displaystyle\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{1}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{2}]W^{a}(q_{1})W^{b}(q_{2})W^{c}(p_{1}-q_{1}-q_{2})\,a^{d}(p_{2})

\displaystyle\sim

(144)

\displaystyle\hskip-258.92024pt-2i\,g_{s}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{1}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptq_{2}]\Big\{f^{ade}\,W^{e}(p_{2}+q_{1})W^{b}(q_{2})W^{c}(p_{1}-q_{1}-q_{2})\bigg[\frac{p_{2}^{\mu}}{p_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-250.38434pt+\,f^{bde}\,W^{a}(q_{1})W^{e}(p_{2}+q_{2})W^{c}(p_{1}-q_{1}-q_{2})\bigg[\frac{p_{2}^{\mu}}{p_{2}^{2}}+\frac{q_{2}^{\mu}}{q_{2}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-250.38434pt+\,f^{cde}\,W^{a}(q_{1})W^{b}(q_{2})W^{e}(p_{1}+p_{2}-q_{1}-q_{2})\bigg[\frac{p_{2}^{\mu}}{p_{2}^{2}}+\frac{(p_{1}-q_{1}-q_{2})^{\mu}}{(p_{1}-q_{1}-q_{2})^{2}}\bigg]\bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{2})+\ldots,

where the ellipses stand for
WWW\to WWWW

and
WWW\to WWWWW
transitions not yet calculated,
which will enter starting at N
3
LL.
The three-to-three Reggeon vertex is shown in
figure
5
.
Note that, as discussed in
[
17
]
,
at this logaritmic accuracy, eqs. (
143
) and (
144
)
corresponds to summing the application of the first
line of eq. (
142
) respectively on
W^{a}
,
W^{b}

and
W^{a}
,
W^{b}
and
W^{c}
, with the Reggeons
not involved in the emission acting as spectators.

Figure 5
:
A Diagram describing the three-to-three Reggeon
vertex associated with the emission of a real gluon.

So far, all the vertices given in eqs. (
142
)
and (
143
) are derived in the framework of the
shockwave formalism to leading order, see eq. (
140
).
In general, each vertex will receive new contributions
beyond the leading order. In particular, the
Reggeon-gluon-Reggeon vertex, which we denote by

{\cal R}g{\cal R}
, has the structure

W^{a}(p_{1})\,a^{b}(p_{2})\sim-2i\,g_{s}\,f^{abc}\,v^{\rm SR}\big[(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2},p_{1}^{2},p_{2}^{2},\tau\big]\,W^{c}(p_{1}+p_{2})\bigg[\frac{p_{1}^{\mu}}{p_{1}^{2}}+\frac{p_{2}^{\mu}}{p_{2}^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{2}),

(145)

where the function
v^{\rm SR}[p_{1}^{2},(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2},p_{2}^{2},\tau]=1+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
identifies loop corrections to
the Lipatov vertex: as we will see below, it
coincides with the function defined in
eq. (
71
), barring
the scheme in which Regge factorization is applied:
as explained following
eqs. (
134
) and (
135
), here, within the context of the multi-Reggeon effective theory, we naturally use the SR/MR scheme, hence the superscript “SR” on
v^{\rm SR}
. In section
8
the vertex will be related, along with the impact factors and Regge trajectory, to the alternative, pole/cut scheme of
[
24
,
23
]
.
Note also that in the present section we use simplified notation,
omitting the polarization index (
\lambda_{4}
) corresponding to
the helicity of the emitted gluon. The one-loop correction
contributes to the
2\to 3
amplitude at NLL in the high-energy
limit; the two-loop corrections will become relevant at NNLL.

6.3
Identifying single Reggeon contributions to
2\to 3
amplitudes

We are now in a position to consider again
the amplitude in eq. (
138
), and
separate single-Reggeon from multiple-Reggeon
exchanges, with the aim to reproduce the Regge
factorisation formula in eq. (
71
).
To this end we split the amplitude in
eq. (
138
) into single Reggeon (SR)
and
Multi-Reggeon
(MR) components:

{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}={\cal\bar{M}}^{\rm SR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}+{\cal\bar{M}}^{\rm MR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}},

(146)

and we decompose the Hamiltonian
H

in eq. (
136
) accordingly, i.e. we
separate the evolution of a single Reggeon
from the remaining Multi-Reggeon components:

\displaystyle H=H_{1\to 1}+H_{\rm MR},\qquad\text{where}\qquad H_{\rm MR}\equiv\sum_{n,m}H_{n\to m}(1-\delta_{n1}\delta_{m1}),

(147)

where we identify the single-Reggeon term

H_{1\to 1}
with the gluon Regge trajectory

\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t)
times the
t
-channel
colour charge
-\mathbf{T}_{t_{i}}^{2}
:

H_{1\to 1}\,\eta_{i}=-\mathbf{T}_{t_{i}}^{2}\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{i})\,\eta_{i}.

(148)

Let us stress once again that the
superscript “SR” indicates that

\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t)
identifies the Regge
trajectory in the SR/MR scheme, as explained following
eqs. (
134
) and (
135
).
The relation will the pole/cut scheme

[
24
,
23
]
,
will be presented in section
8
.

The single Reggeon amplitude involves exclusively
the exchange of a single Reggeon in both the
\eta_{1}

and
\eta_{2}
rapidity spans, thus we have

\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}^{\rm SR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}=\big\langle\psi_{j,1}\big|e^{-H_{1\to 1}\eta_{2}}\,a_{4}(p_{4})\,e^{-H_{1\to 1}\eta_{1}}\big|\psi_{i,1}\big\rangle.

(149)

Instead, the Multi-Reggeon amplitude

{\cal\bar{M}}^{\rm MR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

involves
H_{\rm MR}
in either or both
of the rapidity spans, and we postpone
its analysis to section
7
.
Here we proceed by elaborating the SR component.
Using eq. (
148
), as well as
eqs. (
132
) and (
134
)
in eq. (
138
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal M}^{\rm SR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big[g_{s}\,\mathbf{T}_{i}^{x}\,C^{\rm SR}_{i}(p_{i},p_{i}^{\prime},\tau)\Big]e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{1})\,\eta_{1}}\Big[g_{s}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{y}\,C^{\rm SR}_{j}(p_{j},p_{j}^{\prime},\tau)\Big]e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{2})\,\eta_{2}}

(150)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\,\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,a_{4}(p_{4})\,W^{x}(q_{1})\big|0\big\rangle.

We can now take into account the emission
of a central gluon by means of
eq. (
145
). This gives

\displaystyle\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,a^{a_{4}}_{4}(p_{4})\,W^{x}(q_{1})\big|0\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\,g_{s}\,f^{xa_{4}y^{\prime}}\,v^{\rm SR}(t_{1},t_{2},p_{4}^{2},\tau)\bigg[\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}+\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})

(151)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\,\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,W^{y^{\prime}}(q_{1}+p_{4})\big|0\big\rangle\,.

At this point we are left with the expectation
value in the last line, which simply gives the free
Reggeon propagator
11
11

11

Note that we follow
the convention that Reggeons emitted from the
projectile
i
have incoming momentum, while
Reggeons Absorbed into the target
j
have
outgoing momentum, such that momentum in
the transverse plane flows from
j
to
i
.

[
17
,
18
]
:

\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,W^{y^{\prime}}(q_{1}+p_{4})\big|0\big\rangle=\frac{i\delta^{yy^{\prime}}}{q_{2}^{2}}(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(q_{1}+q_{2}+p_{4})\,.

(152)

Inserting this result into eq. (
151
)
we obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,a^{a_{4}}_{4}(p_{4})\,W^{x}(q_{1})\big|0\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\,g_{s}\,f^{xa_{4}y}\,v(t_{1},t_{2},p_{4}^{2},\tau)\frac{(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(q_{1}+q_{2}+p_{4})}{q_{1}^{2}\,q_{2}^{2}}

(153)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\,\bigg[\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}+\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})\,.

We can easily check that this is indeed
proportional to the tree-level Lipatov
vertex. Taking into account that

v(t_{1},t_{2},p_{4}^{2},\tau)=1+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
,
at tree level we can evaluate
eq. (
153
) in
d=2
, such that the
relation in eq. (
20
) applies;
one has
(q_{1}^{\mu}/q_{1}^{2}+p_{4}^{\mu}/p_{4}^{2})\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})=-1/\sqrt{2}({\bf\bar{q}}_{2}{\bf q_{1}})/{\bf\bar{p}_{4}}
;
enforcing momentum conservation according
to eqs. (
11
) and (
13
)
we get

\displaystyle\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,a^{a_{4}}_{4}(p_{4})\,W^{x}(q_{1})\big|0\big\rangle\big|_{\rm tree}

(154)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=\,\sqrt{2}\,g_{s}\,f^{ya_{4}x}\,\frac{{\bf\bar{p}}_{3}{\bf p_{5}}}{\bf\bar{p}_{4}}\,\frac{(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}({\bf p}_{3}+{\bf p}_{4}+{\bf p}_{5})}{t_{1}\,t_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt=\,g_{s}\,f^{ya_{4}x}V^{(0)}(Q_{1},P_{4}^{\oplus},Q_{2})\,\frac{(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}({\bf p}_{3}+{\bf p}_{4}+{\bf p}_{5})}{t_{1}\,t_{2}}\,,

where in the last line we have identified the
tree-level Lipatov vertex
V^{(0)}=\sqrt{2}\,{\bf\bar{p}}_{3}{\bf p_{5}}/{\bf\bar{p}_{4}}

as given in eq. (
21
).
Beyond tree level, comparing eq. (
153
)
with eq. (
154
) we readily obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle 0\big|W^{y}(-q_{2})\,a^{a_{4}}_{4}(p_{4})\,W^{x}(q_{1})\big|0\big\rangle

(155)

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt=\,g_{s}\,f^{ya_{4}x}\,V^{(0)}(Q_{1},P_{4}^{\oplus},Q_{2})\,v^{\rm SR}(t_{1},t_{2},{\bf p}_{4}^{2},\tau)\,\frac{(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}({\bf p}_{3}+{\bf p}_{4}+{\bf p}_{5})}{t_{1}\,t_{2}}\,.

Inserting eq. (
155
) into eq. (
150
)
we finally obtain

\displaystyle{\cal M}^{\rm SR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2s_{12}\,\Big[g_{s}\,\mathbf{T}_{i}^{x}\,C^{\rm SR}_{i}(p_{i},p_{i}^{\prime},\tau)\Big]e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{1})\,\eta_{1}}\Big[g_{s}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{y}\,C^{\rm SR}_{j}(p_{j},p_{j}^{\prime},\tau)\Big]e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{2})\,\eta_{2}}

(156)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,g_{s}(i\,f^{ya_{4}x})V^{(0)}(Q_{1},P_{4}^{\oplus},Q_{2})\,v^{\rm SR}(t_{1},t_{2},{\bf p}_{4}^{2},\tau)\,\frac{(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}({\bf p}_{3}+{\bf p}_{4}+{\bf p}_{5})}{t_{1}\,t_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{\rm SR}_{i}(p_{i},p_{i^{\prime}},\tau)\,e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{1})\,\eta_{1}}\,v^{\rm SR}(t_{1},t_{2},{\bf p}_{4}^{2},\tau)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\,e^{C_{A}\,\alpha^{\rm SR}_{g}(t_{2})\,\eta_{2}}\,c^{\rm SR}_{j}(p_{j},p_{j^{\prime}},\tau)\,{\cal M}^{\rm tree}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\,,

where in the second line we have identified
the tree-level amplitude according to
eqs. (
14
) and (
22
). We see that the
1-Reggeon transition as obtained within
the shockwave formalism reproduces
the classical Regge factorization formula
of eq. (
71
), when the rapidity factors
are identified as in eq. (
139
).
Notice, however, that within the shockwave
formalism (at least in the present formulation)
one only determines rapidity evolution, therefore
the impact factors and the Lipatov vertex
appears as effective matching coefficients.
In particular, this means that the
shockwave formalism cannot predict the
analytic structure in eq. (
73
).
The impact factors and Lipatov vertex
are not predicted within the theory,
and need to be extracted by matching the
factorization formula on the r.h.s. to
the amplitude on the l.h.s of
eq. (
156
). In this respect,
we see that the contribution due to
the multi-Reggeon transitions in the
last line of eq. (
156
) plays
a relevant role. In particular, they
become essential, starting at NNLL
accuracy, for a consistent extraction
of the Lipatov vertex. The shockwave
formalism provides a method to
systematically compute multi-Reggeon
transitions, expressing them in terms
of integrals which solve iteratively
the Balitsky-JIMWLK evolution equation,
as discussed above. We will compute
explicitly their contribution at one
and two loops in section
7
.
The consistent extraction of the Lipatov
vertex at two loops will be then
discussed in Section
8
.

7
Multi-Reggeon computations

In section
4
we have discussed the factorized structure of the amplitude
in presence of a single Reggeon exchange. In section

6
we presented the formalism for computing multi-Reggeon
exchanges. We now compute their contribution to the
2\to 3
scattering
amplitude. As an illustration, in section
7.1
we calculate the complete
multi-Reggeon contribution at one loop. Subsequently, in section
7.2
we describe the structure of all multi-Reggeon contributions to the two-loop amplitude, and finally in section
7.3
compute explicitly
the respective
(-,-)
component.

7.1
Multi-Reggeon contributions to the one-loop amplitude

The expansion of eq. (
138
) to one
loop shows that there are two possible transitions
involving multiple Reggeons. One entails the
exchange of two Reggeons between the target and
the projectile, as shown in Fig.
6

(a). In this case the emission of the central gluon
is governed by eq. (
143
), whose diagrammatic
representation is given in
Fig.
4
.
The other transition involves the emission of
a single Reggeon from the projectile
i
which
subsequently, upon emission of the central gluon,
is turned into two Reggeons on the target side,
as shown in Fig.
6
(b), or its
symmetric counterpart under target-projectile
interchange.
This transition is governed by the second
line of eq. (
142
), which in turn is represented
diagrammatically by the central diagram in
Fig.
3
. In what follows
we evaluate both transitions in some detail, to be able to discuss the techniques
involved in the calculation.

Figure 6
:
Multi-Reggeon exchange contribution to the one-loop

2\to 3
amplitude. Diagram (a) involves the exchange of two
Reggeons between the target and the projectile, labelled as

{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}
in the main text. Diagram (b)
represents the one-to-two Reggeon transition amplitude,
denoted by
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
in the main text.
At one loop there is an additional diagram, involving the
two-to-one Reggeon transition, or
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}

amplitude. The latter is not shown here, because it is identical to the

{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
amplitude by
target-projectile symmetry.

The
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}
transition.

Using eq. (
127b
) in eq. (
138
) one has

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]

(157)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{2})W^{b^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{2})\,a_{4}^{c}(p_{4})\,W^{a}(k_{1})W^{b}(q_{1}-k_{1})|0\rangle,

Next, using eq. (
143
) we get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\,g_{s}\left(\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]

(158)

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt\times\Bigg\{f^{acd}\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{2})W^{b^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{2})\,W^{d}(k_{1}+p_{4})W^{b}(q_{1}-k_{1})|0\rangle\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{k_{1}^{\mu}}{k_{1}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-113.81102pt+\,f^{bcd}\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{2})W^{b^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{2})\,W^{a}(k_{1})W^{d}(-q_{1}-k_{1}+p_{4})|0\rangle\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\Bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})\,.

We contract a Reggeon in the target
with one in the projectile according to

\big\langle 0\big|W^{x}(p_{1})\,W^{y}(p_{2})\big|0\big\rangle=\frac{i\delta^{xy}}{p_{1}^{2}}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\,\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{1}-p_{2})\,,

(159)

which identifies the momenta defined in the
same orientation. Wick contractions between
two
W
s both originating from
i
or both
originating with
j
lead to vanishing
(scaleless) integrals. Integrating
over
k_{2}
using the
\delta
functions
from the Reggeon propagators gives

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\,g_{s}\left(\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(160)

\displaystyle\hskip-102.43008pt\times\,\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]\Bigg\{\left(f^{acb^{\prime}}\delta^{a^{\prime}b}+f^{aca^{\prime}}\delta^{bb^{\prime}}\right)\frac{1}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}(q_{1}+q_{2}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{k_{1}^{\mu}}{k_{1}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\,\left(f^{bca^{\prime}}\delta^{ab^{\prime}}+f^{bcb^{\prime}}\delta^{aa^{\prime}}\right)\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+q_{2})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\Bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

The kinematic factor in first term in the
curly brackets is equal to the second one,
upon performing the shift of the loop
momentum
q_{1}-k_{1}\to k_{1}
. After this
manipulation we combine terms and get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\,g_{s}\left(\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(161)

\displaystyle\hskip-108.12054pt\times\,\Big(f^{acb^{\prime}}\delta^{a^{\prime}b}+f^{aca^{\prime}}\delta^{bb^{\prime}}+f^{bca^{\prime}}\delta^{ab^{\prime}}+f^{bcb^{\prime}}\delta^{aa^{\prime}}\Big)\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+q_{2})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\Bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

Thus, we showed that the result factorises as a single colour
factor multiplying the entire integral. Defining

\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\{a,b\}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}+\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\right),

(162)

we may write this colour factor as follows:

i\,\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\left(f^{acb^{\prime}}\delta^{a^{\prime}b}+f^{aca^{\prime}}\delta^{bb^{\prime}}+f^{bca^{\prime}}\delta^{ab^{\prime}}+f^{bcb^{\prime}}\delta^{aa^{\prime}}\right)=-4i\,f^{a^{\prime}ca}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\{a,b\}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b\}}\,,

(163)

hence we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8i\pi^{2}\,g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,f^{a^{\prime}ca}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\{a,b\}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b\}}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(164)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\,\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+q_{2})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

As for the case of
2\to 2
amplitude
(see
[
18
,
23
]
),
we want to express this result in terms of loop
integrals and color operators acting on the
tree-level amplitude. Indeed, we observe that
the color structure in eq. (
164
)
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\{a,b\}}\,i\,f^{a^{\prime}ca}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}-\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}+\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)\big[\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,i\,f^{a^{\prime}ca}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\big]

(165)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{T}_{(--)}\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)},

where in the first line we
have used techniques discussed
in Refs.
[
18
,
23
]

to express the color factor in terms of operators
acting on the tree-level color structure. In the
second line we have then identified the operators
with
\mathbf{T}_{(--)}
, defined in eq. (
40
), and
the tree-level colour structure with the factor

\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}
, defined in eq. (
24
).

We can now consider the full amplitude,
switching from
{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

to
{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}
according to
eq. (
138
). We take into account
eq. (
165
) and also
the fact that
Z_{i/j}=1+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
.
Furthermore, in order to evaluate the loop
integral, it is useful to notice that

q_{1}=p_{5}
,
q_{2}=p_{3}
, and momentum
conservation fixes
p_{4}=-p_{3}-p_{5}
.
Also, we work in dimensional regularization
in the
\overline{\rm MS}
scheme, thus
we make now the renormalization scale explicit
by replacing
\alpha_{s}\to\alpha_{s}\big[\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}/(4\pi)\big]^{\epsilon}
. In the
end we get

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4i\pi^{2}\,s_{12}\,g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,C_{i}^{(0)}C_{j}^{(0)}\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{3}+p_{4}+p_{5})

(166)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\,\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

The loop integration can be reduced to a
set of master integrals by means of IBPs.
One has

\displaystyle\pi\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})

(167)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=\,\Bigg\{\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,1,0)}}{p_{3}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{p_{3}\cdot p_{5}\,p_{3}^{\mu}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\bigg]+\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,0,1)}}{2p_{5}^{2}}\frac{p_{5}^{2}\,p_{3}^{\mu}-p_{3}\cdot p_{5}\,p_{5}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-213.39566pt-\,\frac{{\cal I}_{(0,1,1)}}{2p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\frac{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5}+p_{5}^{2})p_{3}^{\mu}-(p_{3}^{2}+p_{3}\cdot p_{5})p_{5}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-213.39566pt+\,\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,1,1)}}{2p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\frac{\big[2(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}+p_{3}^{2}(p_{3}\cdot p_{5}-p_{5}^{2})\big]p_{5}^{\mu}-(p_{3}^{2}+p_{3}\cdot p_{5})p_{5}^{2}\,p_{3}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\Bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}),

where we have introduced the family of master integrals

{\cal I}_{(a,b,c)}=\pi\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk]\,\frac{\left(p_{5}^{2}\right)^{b}\left(p_{3}^{2}\right)^{c}}{\big[k^{2}\big]^{a}\big[(k+p_{3})^{2}\big]^{b+b^{\prime}\epsilon}\big[(k-p_{5})^{2}\big]^{c+c^{\prime}\epsilon}}.

(168)

All the scalar products on the r.h.s. of
eq. (
167
) can be evaluated in two
transverse dimension by means of eqs. (
10
),
(
11
) and (
20
),
except for the integrals
{\cal I}_{(a,b,c)}

which needs to be evaluated in dimensional
regularization in
d=2-2\epsilon
. Inserting
eq. (
167
) into eq. (
166
),
after some elaboration we get

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\frac{1}{z-\bar{z}}\bigg[(1-\bar{z})\,{\cal I}_{(1,1,0)}+z\,{\cal I}_{(1,0,1)}-\frac{{\cal I}_{(0,1,1)}}{|{\bf p}_{4}|^{2}\bar{z}(1-z)}-{\cal I}_{(1,1,1)}\Big]

(169)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}\,,

where we have identified

{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}

according to eq. (
22
).

Eq. (
169
) contains three bubble integrals
and a triangle integral. The bubbles are easy
to calculate by means of the formula

B_{a,b}^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p^{2},\epsilon)=\pi\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk]\,\frac{1}{[k^{2}]^{a}[(k+p)^{2}]^{b}}=\frac{B_{a,b}(\epsilon)}{2\epsilon}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{p^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}(p^{2})^{1-a-b},

(170)

where

B_{a,b}(\epsilon)=2\epsilon\frac{e^{\epsilon\gamma_{E}}}{4}\frac{\Gamma(1-a-\epsilon)\Gamma(1-b-\epsilon)\Gamma(a+b-1+\epsilon)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(2-2\epsilon-a-b)}.

(171)

It is easy to identify

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{(1,1,0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{3}^{2}\,B_{(1,1)}^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{3}^{2},\epsilon),

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{(1,0,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{5}^{2}\,B_{(1,1)}^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{5}^{2},\epsilon),

(172)

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{(0,1,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}\,B_{(1,1)}^{(2-2\epsilon)}[(p_{3}+p_{5})^{2},\epsilon]=p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}\,B_{(1,1)}^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}^{2},\epsilon).

The triangle integral for generic powers
a
,
b
,

c
has been provided to all order in
\epsilon
in

[
114
,
115
]
. Here
we need the case
a=b=c=1
, which allows
for some simplification. In the end one has

\displaystyle{\cal I}_{(1,1,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk]\,\frac{p_{5}^{2}\,p_{3}^{2}}{k^{2}\,(k+p_{3})^{2}\,(k-p_{5})^{2}}

(173)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\epsilon}\Gamma(1-\epsilon)\Gamma(1+\epsilon)^{2}}{4\epsilon}(z-\bar{z})\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}(z-\bar{z})^{2}}{z\bar{z}(1-z)(1-\bar{z})p_{4}^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\epsilon}(1+2\epsilon)\Gamma(1-\epsilon)^{2}\Gamma(1+\epsilon)}{2\epsilon(1+\epsilon)\Gamma(1-2\epsilon)}\,z\bar{z}(1-z)(1-\bar{z})\,\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{p_{4}^{2}}\bigg)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\bigg\{\frac{1}{z(1-\bar{z})}\,_{2}F_{1}\bigg(1,-\epsilon,2+\epsilon,\frac{\bar{z}(1-z)}{z(1-\bar{z})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\,\frac{(z\bar{z})^{-1-\epsilon}}{z-\bar{z}}\,_{2}F_{1}\bigg(1,2(1+\epsilon),2+\epsilon,-\frac{1-z}{z-\bar{z}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\,\frac{[(1-z)(1-\bar{z})]^{-1-\epsilon}}{z-\bar{z}}\,_{2}F_{1}\bigg(1,2(1+\epsilon),2+\epsilon,-\frac{\bar{z}}{z-\bar{z}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

Inserting eq. (
173
) as well as
the expression for the bubble integrals
from eq. (
170
) into eq. (
169
),
expanding in powers of
\epsilon
, after
some elaboration one has

{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}=i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\,G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree},

(174)

where the function
G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}
reads

\displaystyle G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)+\epsilon\bigg[-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}-2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})

(175)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\,\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{2}),

with
|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}=z\bar{z}|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}
,

|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}=(1-z)(1-\bar{z})|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}

and the function
D_{2}(z,\bar{z})
represents a
single-valued combination of dilogarithms

D_{2}(z,\bar{z})={\rm Li}_{2}(z)-{\rm Li}_{2}(\bar{z})+\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{1-z}{1-\bar{z}}\bigg)\ln(z\bar{z}),

(176)

which is referred to as the
Bloch-Wigner dilogarithm
[
116
]
.

Before concluding, it is instructive to
have an explicit look at the action of the
color operator
\mathbf{T}_{(--)}
on the tree
level amplitude
{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}
.
Let us recall that in color space the
amplitude is written as a vector, according
to eq. (
26
). An operator
\mathbf{T}_{X}

then acts as a matrix, according to (see
appendix
B.5
for more details)

\sum_{ji}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{X}^{[j][i]}\,{\cal M}^{[i]}=\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,{\cal M}^{[j]}.

(177)

Decomposing the amplitude on an
orthonormal basis in the
t
-channel,
as in eq. (
34
),
the tree level amplitudes
{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}

are given by a single element: explicitly one has

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{[8,8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}},

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}},

(178)

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}\,\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]},

where the basis elements
c^{[r_{1},r_{2}]_{r}}

for the respective scattering processes
are defined in eqs. (
325
), (
335
),
(
338
) and (
341
), and the explicit
coefficients
\big({\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[J]}

are provided in eq. (
347
). Taking
this into account, the action of the operator

\mathbf{T}_{(--)}
on these tree level amplitudes
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{[j][8,8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}^{2}-4}{2N_{c}}\,c^{[8,8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}},

(179)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{[j][8,8_{a}]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8,8_{s}]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}},

(180)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{[j][8_{a},8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8_{s},8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}},

(181)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{[j][8_{a},8_{a}]}\,\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}-3}{2N_{c}}}\,c^{[0,0]}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}+3}{2N_{c}}}\,c^{[27,27]}

(182)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\,\frac{N_{c}}{4}\,c^{[8_{s},8_{s}]}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}.

Notice that for completeness we provide both the case
in which
i=q
,
j=g
, namely
{\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}
,
and the case in which
i=g
,
j=q
, i.e.

{\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}
. As it is evident, the action
of
\mathbf{T}_{(--)}
on these amplitudes is obviously symmetric.
Comparing with the color basis in eq. (
341
)
it is interesting to note that, for the
gg\to ggg

amplitude, the color operator
\mathbf{T}_{(--)}
projects
onto the color basis elements which are even both
under
1\leftrightarrow 5
and
2\leftrightarrow 3
,
as expected from Bose symmetry.

The
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}

and
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}
transitions.

At one loop there are two additional contributions,
namely, the one-to-two (
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
) and
the two-to-one (
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}
) Reggeon
transition amplitudes. These transitions are related
by the target-projectile (or top-bottom) symmetry,
therefore we focus on the calculation of the

{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
amplitude, represented
in Fig.
6
(b). In the end
we will obtain the
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}

amplitude by symmetry.

The
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
amplitude
at one loop reads

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\,g_{s}\,\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{2}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]

(183)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{2})W^{b^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{2})\,a_{4}^{c}(p_{4})\,W^{a}(q_{1})|0\rangle.

In this case we need the second line of
eq. (
142
), represented by the central
diagram in Fig.
3
.
We get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{s}^{5}}{2}\,f^{cc^{\prime}e}f^{ead}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{j}^{b^{\prime}}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]

(184)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\times\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{2})W^{b^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{2})\,W^{c^{\prime}}(q_{1}-k_{1}+p_{4})W^{d}(k_{1})|0\rangle\bigg[\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}-\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})\,.

Contracting the Reggeon fields according to
eq. (
159
) and integrating over
k_{2}

using the
\delta
functions from the Reggeon
propagators, after some elaboration gives

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\pi^{2}\,g^{3}_{s}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,f^{a^{\prime}ce}f^{eab^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(185)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\,\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+q_{2})^{2}}\Bigg[\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}+\frac{(k_{1}-q_{1})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-q_{1})^{2}}\Bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}),

where we have expressed the color structure
on the line
i
in terms of the operator
defined in eq. (
162
). As for the

{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}
transition
amplitude, we want to write the result in
eq. (
185
) in terms of
loop integrals and color operators
acting on the tree level amplitude.
To this end we first use the Jacobi
identity as follows:

f^{eab^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}=i\big(\mathbf{T}_{i}^{e}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b^{\prime}}-\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{e}\big).

(186)

Inserting this result into the full color
structure of eq. (
185
),
and proceeding as before with methods
described in
[
18
,
23
]

we obtain

\displaystyle if^{a^{\prime}ce}\,\big(\mathbf{T}_{i}^{e}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b^{\prime}}-\mathbf{T}_{i}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{e}\big)\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}-\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}\big)\big[\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,i\,f^{a^{\prime}ca}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\big]

(187)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)},

where in the second line we have identified
the color operators in the first line with

\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}
, defined in eq. (
40
), and
the tree-level colour structure with the factor

\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}
, defined in eq. (
24
).

We can now consider the full amplitude,
switching from
{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

to
{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}
according to
eq. (
138
). Taking into account
eq. (
165
), and also that

Z_{i/j}=1+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
, at one loop we
have

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4i\pi^{2}\,s_{12}\,g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,C_{i}^{(0)}C_{j}^{(0)}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(188)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\,\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+q_{2})^{2}}\Bigg[\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}+\frac{(k_{1}-q_{1})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-q_{1})^{2}}\Bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

As before, we set
q_{1}=p_{5}
,

q_{2}=p_{3}
, and make the renormalization
scale explicit by replacing
\alpha_{s}\to\alpha_{s}\big[\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}/(4\pi)\big]^{\epsilon}
,
obtaining

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4i\pi^{2}\,s_{12}\,g_{s}^{3}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,C_{i}^{(0)}C_{j}^{(0)}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{3}+p_{4}+p_{5})

(189)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\,\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{5}^{\mu}}{p_{5}^{2}}+\frac{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

The loop integration in
eq. (
189
) can be reduced
to a set of master integrals by means
of IBPs. In this case we obtain

\displaystyle\pi\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}}}{4\pi}\bigg)^{\epsilon}\int\frac{[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}]}{k_{1}^{2}(k_{1}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{5}^{\mu}}{p_{5}^{2}}+\frac{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})

(190)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=\,\Bigg\{\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,1,0)}}{2p_{3}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{2(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\frac{p_{5}^{\mu}}{p_{5}^{2}}-\frac{p_{3}\cdot p_{5}\,p_{3}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-213.39566pt+\,\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,0,1)}}{2p_{5}^{2}}\frac{p_{3}\cdot p_{5}\,p_{5}^{\mu}-p_{5}^{2}\,p_{3}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}+\frac{{\cal I}_{(0,1,1)}}{2p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\frac{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5}+p_{5}^{2})p_{3}^{\mu}-(p_{3}^{2}+p_{3}\cdot p_{5})p_{5}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip-213.39566pt+\,\frac{{\cal I}_{(1,1,1)}}{2p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\frac{(p_{3}^{2}+p_{3}\cdot p_{5})p_{5}^{2}p_{3}^{\mu}-\big[2(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}+p_{3}^{2}(p_{3}\cdot p_{5}-p_{5}^{2})\big]p_{5}^{\mu}}{(p_{3}\cdot p_{5})^{2}-p_{3}^{2}\,p_{5}^{2}}\Bigg\}\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}),

where the family of integrals is the
same introduced in eq. (
168
). All
the scalar products on the r.h.s. of
eq. (
190
) can be evaluated in two
transverse dimension by means of eqs. (
10
),
(
11
) and (
20
),
except for the integrals
{\cal I}_{(a,b,c)}

which needs to be evaluated in dimensional
regularization in
d=2-2\epsilon
. Inserting
eq. (
190
) into eq. (
189
),
after some elaboration we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\,\frac{1}{z-\bar{z}}\Big[(1-z)\,{\cal I}_{(1,1,0)}+z\,{\cal I}_{(1,0,1)}

(191)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\,\frac{{\cal I}_{(0,1,1)}}{|{\bf p}_{4}|^{2}\bar{z}(1-z)}-{\cal I}_{(1,1,1)}\Big]\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}.

Inserting eq. (
173
) as well as
the expression for the bubble integrals
from eq. (
170
) into eq. (
191
),
expanding in powers of
\epsilon
we arrive at

{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}=i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\,G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree},

(192)

where

\displaystyle G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)+\epsilon\bigg[-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}+2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})

(193)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt+\,\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{2}).

As for the
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}

transition, it is instructive to have an
explicit look at the action of the
color operator
\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}
on the tree
level amplitude
{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}
.
Using the same notation as in eq. (
165
),
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{[j][8,8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\sqrt{2}\,c^{[8,1]}

(194)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\,\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8,8]_{s}}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{[j][8,8_{a}]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{2N_{c}}{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-1}}\,c^{[8,1]}+\frac{N_{c}}{2}\,c^{[8,8_{s}]_{s}}

(195)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\,\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}+3}{N_{c}+1}}\,c^{[8,27]}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}-3}{N_{c}-1}}\,c^{[8,0]}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{[j][8_{a},8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\sqrt{2}\,c^{[8_{a},1]}+\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8_{a},8]_{s}}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{[j][8_{a},8_{a}]}\,\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{2N_{c}}{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-1}}\,c^{[8_{a},1]}+\frac{N_{c}}{2}\,c^{[8_{a},8_{s}]}

(197)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\,\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}+3}{N_{c}+1}}\,c^{[8_{a},27]}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}-3}{N_{c}-1}}\,c^{[8_{a},0]}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}.

Comparing with the color basis in eq. (
341
)
it is interesting to note that, for the
gg\to ggg

amplitude, the color operator
\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}
projects
onto the color basis elements which are odd under

1\leftrightarrow 5
and even under
2\leftrightarrow 3
,
as expected from Bose symmetry.

With the result for the
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}

in eq. (
192
), we can immediately
obtain the
{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}
transition
amplitude by applying projectile-target symmetry.
For the color operator, the symmetry implies

\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\to\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}
, while the kinematic
dependence is obtained by applying the
target-projectile symmetry
z\to 1-\bar{z}
,

\bar{z}\to 1-z
to eq. (
192
).
Taking into account the property

D_{2}(1-\bar{z},1-z)=D_{2}(z,\bar{z}),

(198)

we have

{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}}=i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\,G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})\,\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree},

(199)

where

\displaystyle G^{(1)}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)+\epsilon\bigg[-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}+2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})

(200)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt-\,\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{2}).

The action of the
\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}
color
operator on the tree level reads

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{[j][8,8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\sqrt{2}\,c^{[1,8]}+\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8,8]_{s}}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}},

(201)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{[j][8,8_{a}]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\sqrt{2}\,c^{[1,8_{a}]}+\frac{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-4}}{2}\,c^{[8,8_{a}]_{s}}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{[j][8_{a},8]_{a}}\,\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\frac{2N_{c}}{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-1}}\,c^{[1,8]}+\frac{N_{c}}{2}\,c^{[8_{s},8]_{s}}

(203)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\,\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}+3}{N_{c}+1}}\,c^{[27,8]}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}-3}{N_{c}-1}}\,c^{[0,8]}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{[j][8_{a},8_{a}]}\,\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{2N_{c}}{\sqrt{N_{c}^{2}-1}}\,c^{[1,8_{a}]}+\frac{N_{c}}{2}\,c^{[8_{s},8_{a}]}

(204)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\,\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}+3}{N_{c}+1}}\,c^{[27,8_{a}]}+\sqrt{\frac{N_{c}-3}{N_{c}-1}}\,c^{[0,8_{a}]}\bigg)\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}.

As expected, for the
gg\to ggg

amplitude, the color operator
\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}
projects
onto the color basis elements which are even under

1\leftrightarrow 5
and odd under
2\leftrightarrow 3
,
as expected from Bose symmetry.

The complete MR amplitude at one loop.

The full one-loop multiple-Reggeon contribution is given
by the sum of eqs. (
174
), (
192
) and (
199
), and reads

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}^{\rm MR}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}+{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}+{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 1-loop}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}}

(205)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt=\,i\pi\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\,\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Big[\mathbf{T}_{(--)}+\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}+\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(--)}+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}+\ln\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt+\,\epsilon\Bigg[\Bigg(-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}-2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(--)}

\displaystyle\hskip-59.75095pt+\,\Bigg(-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}+2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})-\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}

\displaystyle\hskip-59.75095pt+\,\Bigg(-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}+2D_{2}(z,\bar{z})+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{3}|^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\ln^{2}\bigg(\frac{\mu^{2}}{|\mathbf{p}_{5}|^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg)\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\,{\cal O}(\epsilon^{2})\Bigg\}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}^{\rm tree}.

7.2
Multi-Reggeon contributions to the two-loop amplitude

Figure 7
:
Multi-Reggeon exchange contribution to
two-loop even-even amplitude
{\cal M}^{(+,+)}
.
Adopting the notation from
[
18
,
21
,
23
]
,
the horizontal gluon in the ladder represents one
application of the BFKL kernel, while the blob
on the upper line
j
represents the two-Reggeon
impact factor. There are two additional diagrams,
in which the BFKL kernel is applied below the
emission vertex, and one where the Two-Reggeon
impact factor is taken on the leg
i
.

We are now ready to consider the amplitude at two
loops. At this order we need to take into account
several multi-Reggeon transitions, which contribute
to all the amplitude components
{\cal M}^{(\pm,\pm)}
.
As discussed in the introduction, purpose of this
paper is to disentangle the Regge pole and cut
contribution, such as to achieve an unambiguous
definition of the Litpatov vertex at two loops.
To this end we need to focus on the multi-Reggeon
contribution to
{\cal M}^{(-,-)}
only, that we
will calculate in detail in what follows. For
illustration, however, let us start by identifying
all the contributions that appear at two loops.

As usual, we obtain such terms by expanding
eq. (
138
) to second order in the strong
coupling constant. We recall at this point that
the signature symmetry, i.e. the symmetry of the
amplitude under the exchange of
1\leftrightarrow 5

for the projectile
i
, and
2\leftrightarrow 3
for
the target
j
, is in direct correspondence with the
parity of the number of Reggeons emitted: one Reggeon
belong to the odd (
-
) sector, two Reggeons to the
even (
+
) sector, three Reggeons to the odd sector
again, and so on. Following this criterion, we can
unambiguously assign all contributions to
a specific amplitude component
{\cal M}^{(\pm,\pm)}
.
Starting from the even-even amplitude, or

{\cal M}^{(+,+)}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}^{(+,+)}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{2}g{\cal R}^{2}}

(206)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta_{2}\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|H_{2\to 2}\,a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,2}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}-\eta_{1}\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\,H_{2\to 2}\big|\psi_{i,2}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}

\displaystyle+\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,2}\big\rangle^{\text{NLO}}.

In this equation, the term proportional to
\eta_{2}

involves an application of the leading order BFKL kernel

H_{2\to 2}
between the target
j
and the emission of
the central gluon, as represented in Fig.
7

(a); in the second term, proportional to
\eta_{1}
, the BFKL
kernel is applied between the central emission gluon and
the projectile
i
. The last term, instead, involves the
sum of three NLO contributions: the first is given by the
insertion of the one-loop two-Reggeon impact factor
on the target, as in Figure Fig.
7

(b); the second contribution is obtained by inserting
the two-Reggeon impact factor at one loop on the
projectile; the third term is given by the one-loop
version of the two-Reggeon-gluon emission vertex in
eq. (
143
), which is yet to be calculated. Notice that
the two terms involving
H_{2\to 2}
in eq. (
206
)
contributes at NLL to the amplitude, as evident from the
rapidity factors
\eta_{1}
,
\eta_{2}
multiplying them, while
the last term contributes at NNLL.

Figure 8
:
Multi-Reggeon exchange contribution to
the two-loop odd-even amplitude
{\cal M}^{(-,+)}
.
A small
t
indicates that the corresponding vertex
is taken at tree level, while the label
1l
indicates
that one needs to consider the corresponding insertion
at one loop. The two-loop even-odd amplitude

{\cal M}^{(+,-)}
can be obtained from the
diagrams above by exploiting the target-projectile
(or top-bottom) symmetry.

Next, we consider the two-loop odd-even amplitude
{\cal M}^{(-,+)}
.
In contrast to the one-loop case,
where this amplitude only receives a
contribution from the

{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}
transition, at two loops we also need to take
into account the transition
{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{2}
,
which involves three Reggeons emission from the target. This is in
correspondence to what happens in case of the
2\to 2

scattering amplitudes: in that case, in fact, in the
odd sector
{\cal M}_{ij\to ij}^{(-)}
, starting
at two loops one needs to take into account the mixing
between one and three Reggeons
[
18
]
.
One has thus

\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}^{(-,+)}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}=\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\bigg({\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}+{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{2}}\bigg),

(207)

where in turn the two transitions reads

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta_{2}\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|\,a_{4}(p_{4})H_{1\to 1}\big|\psi_{i,1}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}-\eta_{1}\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|H_{2\to 2}\,a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,1}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}

(208)

\displaystyle+\,\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,1}\big\rangle^{\text{NLO}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\langle\psi_{j,2}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,3}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}.

The first term in eq. (
208
) is represented
in fig.
8
(a), and involves
the insertion of the
H_{1\to 1}
evolution kernel
(i.e., the Regge trajectory at one loop) between
the projectile and the gluon emission. The second term
is represented by fig.
8

(b), and involves the insertion of the leading order
BFKL kernel
H_{2\to 2}
between the central
emission gluon and the target
j
.
The third term denoted by the superscript NLO
involves three contributions: the first term
is represented in fig.
8

(c), and involves the insertion of the one loop
single Reggeon impact factor on the projectile; the
second term, represented in fig.
8

(d), is obtained by inserting the two-Reggeon
impact factor on the target. Last,
the third term is represented in
fig.
8
(e),
and involves the one- to two-Reggeon-gluon
emission vertex at one loop, which, as in
case of the two-Reggeon-gluon emission vertex
in Fig.
7
, is yet to be
calculated, and it would involve the BFLK
kernel at NLO. The term in eq. (
7.2
)
is given instead by the three- to
two-Reggeon-gluon emission vertex.
This term can be obtained either by
expanding eq. (
143
) one additional
order in
g_{s}
, thus taking into account
the three-gluon terms on the r.h.s. of the
equation; alternatively, this term is simply
given by all possible combinations of the
one- to -two-Reggeon-gluon vertex given
in the second line of eq. (
142
) (central
diagram in Fig.
3
),
with an additional Reggeon propagating between
the target and the projectile.

Figure 9
:
Multi-Reggeon exchange contribution to
the two-loop odd-even amplitude
{\cal M}^{(-,-)}
.

Last, we have the odd-odd amplitude, namely
{\cal M}^{(-,-)}
.
In this case we need to take into account
one-to-three, three-to-one and three-to three Reggeon
transitions, on top of the single-Reggeon transition
we described in section
6.3
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}^{(-,-)}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\bigg({\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}}+{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}}

(209)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\,{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}}+{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\bigg).

The first term in this equation represents the single
Reggeon transition, namely, considering the complete
amplitude we have

{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}}={\cal M}^{\rm SR}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}},

(210)

which can be obtained by expanding
eq. (
156
) consistently to second
order in the strong coupling constant.
The second term in eq. (
209
)
reads

\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}}=\big\langle\psi_{j,3}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,1}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}},

(211)

and is represented in Fig.
9

(b), where the single-Reggeon to three-Reggeon-gluon
vertex is given by the last term in eq. (
142
),
also represented by the diagram on the right in
Fig.
3
. Next, the
third term reads

\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}}=\big\langle\psi_{j,1}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,3}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}},

(212)

and can be obtained by target-projectile symmetry
from the
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}
transition
that we just described. Last, the term involving
three Reggeons reads

\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}=\big\langle\psi_{j,3}\big|a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,3}\big\rangle^{\text{LO}}.

(213)

It is represented in Fig.
9

(a), and is obtained by using eq. (
144
)
onto
a_{4}(p_{4})\big|\psi_{i,3}\big\rangle
.
In the following section we proceed to evaluate
explicitly the terms giving rise eq. (
209
).

7.3
Odd-odd Amplitude

The
{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}
transition.

We start with the calculation of the three-Reggeon
transition amplitude. Inserting eq. (
127c
) into
eq. (
208
) one has

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\ {\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{g_{s}^{3}}{6}\right)^{2}(\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}\,(\mathbf{T}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{c^{\prime}})_{j}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{3}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{4}]

(214)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\times\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{3})W^{b^{\prime}}(k_{4})W^{c^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\,a_{4}^{d}(p_{4})\,W^{a}(k_{1})W^{b}(k_{2})W^{c}(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})|0\rangle,

Using eq. (
144
) with
q_{1}=k_{1}
,

q_{2}=k_{2}
,
p_{1}=q_{1}
,
p_{2}=p_{4}
,
we get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\ {\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\,g_{s}\left(\frac{g_{s}^{3}}{6}\right)^{2}(\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}\,(\mathbf{T}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{c^{\prime}})_{j}

(215)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\times\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{3}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{4}]\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{3})W^{b^{\prime}}(k_{4})W^{c^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\times\Bigg\{f^{ade}\,\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{k_{1}^{\mu}}{k_{1}^{2}}\bigg]W^{e}(k_{1}+p_{4})W^{b}(k_{2})W^{c}(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})|0\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-79.6678pt+\,f^{bde}\,\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{k_{2}^{\mu}}{k_{2}^{2}}\bigg]W^{a}(k_{1})W^{e}(k_{2}+p_{4})W^{c}(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})|0\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip-79.6678pt+\,f^{cde}\,\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}+\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{2}-k_{2})^{2}}\bigg]W^{a}(k_{1})W^{b}(k_{2})W^{c}(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2}+p_{4})|0\rangle\Bigg\}\,\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})\,.

From here one proceeds as illustrated
in the previous sections, namely, we
contract the Reggeon fields according
to eq. (
159
), which gives rise
to three Dirac deltas for each terms;
subsequently we integrate over
k_{3}

and
k_{4}
, thus enforcing momentum
conservation. In the end one can exploit
the freedom to shift the remaining two
integration variables, i.e.
k_{1}
and

k_{2}
, to express the momentum
dependent part in a compact form.
After some work one gets

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,\frac{16\pi^{4}}{3}\,g^{3}_{s}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\right)^{2}(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(216)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\times\,\Big\{(\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}+(\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}+(\mathbf{T}^{c}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{a})_{i}\Big\}\,(if^{a^{\prime}da})\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b,c\}}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\times\,\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2}+q_{2})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{1}+k_{2}-q_{1})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}+k_{2}-q_{1})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}),

where, in analogy to eq. (
162
),
we have used the notation

\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\{a,b,c\}}=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{3}}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\sigma(a)}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\sigma(b)}\mathbf{T}_{i}^{\sigma(c)}.

(217)

In order to express this result in terms of
loop integrals and color operators acting on
the tree level amplitude, we first notice
that the color structure in eq. (
216
)
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\Big\{(\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}+(\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{a}\mathbf{T}^{c})_{i}+(\mathbf{T}^{c}\mathbf{T}^{b}\mathbf{T}^{a})_{i}\Big\}\,(if^{a^{\prime}da})\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{a^{\prime},b,c\}}

(218)

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=\,\frac{1}{6}\bigg\{\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}\big(+2\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}-\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}-\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\,\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}\big(-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}+2\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}+\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}-\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\,\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big(-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}+2\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}-\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\,\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big(-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}-\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}+2\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-199.16928pt+\,\frac{C_{A}}{2}\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}+\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)\big(\mathbf{T}_{2}+\mathbf{T}_{3}\big)\bigg\}\big[\mathbf{T}_{i}^{a}\,if^{a^{\prime}da}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{a^{\prime}}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip-227.62204pt=\,\frac{1}{48}\bigg[9\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(++)}^{2}+3\Big(\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{2}\Big)+4C_{A}\mathbf{T}_{(++)}\bigg]\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}.

Inserting this result into eq. (
216
),
switching from
{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}

to
{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}
according to
eq. (
138
), taking into account
momentum conservation and the fact that

Z_{i/j}=1+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
, at two loops
we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi^{4}}{9}\,s_{12}\,g^{3}_{s}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\right)^{2}\,(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{3}+p_{4}+p_{5})\,C_{i}^{(0)}C_{j}^{(0)}

(219)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,\bigg[9\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(++)}^{2}+3\Big(\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{2}\Big)+4C_{A}\mathbf{T}_{(++)}\bigg]\,\mathcal{C}_{ij}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{1}+k_{2}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}+k_{2}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}),

This expression can be elaborates such that
one of the two loop integrals reduces to
a simple bubble integral, of the form given
in eq. (
170
), and the second is given in
terms of the same family of integrals defined
in eq. (
168
). To this end, we shift
k_{1}+k_{2}\to k_{2}
,
then perform the
k_{1}
bubble integral,
obtaining

\displaystyle\pi^{2}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{1}+k_{2}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{1}+k_{2}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})

(220)

\displaystyle\hskip-284.52756pt=\,\pi\frac{\mu^{2\epsilon}B_{1,1}(\epsilon)}{2\epsilon}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\frac{1}{(k_{2}^{2})^{1+\epsilon}(k_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\bigg[\frac{p_{4}^{\mu}}{p_{4}^{2}}-\frac{(k_{2}-p_{5})^{\mu}}{(k_{2}-p_{5})^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

The integral over
k_{2}
can be reduced
by means of IBPs to a set of master
integrals belonging to the topology
in eq. (
168
).
After some elaboration we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(i\pi)^{2}\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\right)^{2}\,\frac{\mu^{2\epsilon}B_{1,1}(\epsilon)}{2\epsilon}\frac{1}{18(z-\bar{z})}

(221)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,\bigg\{(1-\bar{z})\,{\cal I}_{(1+\epsilon,1,0)}+z\,{\cal I}_{(1+\epsilon,0,1)}-\frac{{\cal I}_{(\epsilon,1,1)}}{|{\bf p}_{4}|^{2}\bar{z}(1-z)}-{\cal I}_{(1+\epsilon,1,1)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\,\bigg[9\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(++)}^{2}+3\Big(\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{2}\Big)+4C_{A}\mathbf{T}_{(++)}\bigg]\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}.

The first two master integrals are
easily evaluated by means of eq. (
170
),
while the last two integrals require a
generalization of eq. (
173
).
For conciseness, let us define the
color operator involved in the

{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}
transition
as follows:

{\bf{\cal C}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\equiv 9\mathbf{T}_{(--)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(++)}^{2}+3\Big(\mathbf{T}_{(+-)}^{2}+\mathbf{T}_{(-+)}^{2}\Big)+4C_{A}\mathbf{T}_{(++)}.

(222)

After some elaboration we obtain

{\cal M}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}=(i\pi)^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\right)^{2}\,G^{(2)}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},\mu^{2})\,{\bf{\cal C}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\,{\cal M}_{ij\to igj}^{\rm tree}.

(223)

where, setting for simplicity
\mu^{2}=|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2}
,

G^{(2)}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}
reads

\displaystyle G^{(2)}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}(z,\bar{z},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2},|\mathbf{p}_{4}|^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{288}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{2}{\epsilon}\log\big[z\bar{z}(1-z)(1-\bar{z})\big]-6\,D_{2}(z,\bar{z})-\zeta_{2}

(224)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt+\,2\log^{2}\big[z\bar{z}\big]+2\log^{2}\big[(1-z)(1-\bar{z})\big]+\log\big[z\bar{z}\big]\log\big[(1-z)(1-\bar{z})\big]+{\cal O}(\epsilon)\bigg\}.

To conclude the evaluation of the

{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}
transition,
we report the explicit action of the
color operator on the tree level
amplitude, using the same notation
used in eqs. (
165
) and (
187
):
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,{\bf{\cal C}}^{[j][8,8]_{a}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(2N_{c}^{2}-12+\frac{36}{N_{c}^{2}}\bigg)c^{[8,8]_{a}}\big({\cal M}_{qq\to qgq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8]_{a}},

(225)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,{\bf{\cal C}}^{[j][8,8_{a}]_{a}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\,\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(2N_{c}^{2}+12\big)c^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}}\big({\cal M}_{qg\to qgg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8,8_{a}]_{a}},

(226)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,{\bf{\cal C}}^{[j][8_{a},8]_{a}}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\,\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(2N_{c}^{2}+12\big)c^{[8_{a},8]_{a}}\big({\cal M}_{gq\to ggq}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8]_{a}},

(227)

\displaystyle\sum_{j}c^{[j]}\,{\bf{\cal C}}^{[j][8_{a},8_{a}]}_{{\cal R}^{3}g{\cal R}^{3}}\,\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\big(2N_{c}^{2}+72\big)c^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}

(228)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\,36\sqrt{N_{c}-4}\,c^{[10,\overline{10}]_{1}}\bigg]\big({\cal M}_{gg\to ggg}^{\rm tree}\big)^{[8_{a},8_{a}]}.

The
{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}
transition.

We continue with the Reggeon to three Reggeon
transition, represented by the diagram (b) in
Fig.
9
, which, to the best
of our knowledge, has not been considered in
previous works. This transition involve the
state eq. (
127c
) for the target
j
and the
state eq. (
127a
) for the projectile
i
.
Inserting these states into eq. (
211
)
we have

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\ {\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{s}\left(\frac{g_{s}^{3}}{6}\right)\mathbf{T}^{a}_{i}\,(\mathbf{T}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{c^{\prime}})_{j}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]

(229)

\displaystyle\hskip-99.58464pt\times\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{3})W^{b^{\prime}}(k_{4})W^{c^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\,a_{4}^{d}(p_{4})\,W^{a}(q_{1})|0\rangle.

Using the third line of eq. (
142
) with
p_{1}=q_{1}
,

p_{2}=p_{4}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}\ {\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\,\frac{g_{s}^{7}}{36}\,\,f^{dgx}f^{xfy}f^{yae}\mathbf{T}^{a}_{i}\,(\mathbf{T}^{a^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbf{T}^{c^{\prime}})_{j}\,\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{3}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{4}]

(230)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\times\,\langle 0|W^{a^{\prime}}(k_{3})W^{b^{\prime}}(k_{4})W^{c^{\prime}}(-q_{2}-k_{3}-k_{4})\,W^{g}(q_{1}+p_{4}-k_{1}-k_{2})W^{f}(k_{1})W^{e}(k_{2})|0\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\,\bigg[\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}}-3\frac{(q_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{2})^{2}}+2\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4})\,.

From here we contract the Reggeon fields
according to eq. (
159
); this
gives rise to three Dirac deltas for
each terms, and subsequently we integrate
over
k_{3}
and
k_{4}
, thus enforcing momentum
conservation. In the end we exploit
the freedom to shift the remaining two
integration variables, i.e.
k_{1}
and

k_{2}
, to express the momentum
dependent part in a compact form.
After some work we get

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2s_{12}}{\cal\bar{M}}_{ij\to i^{\prime}gj^{\prime}}\Big|^{\mbox{\scriptsize 2-loops}}_{{\cal R}g{\cal R}^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi^{4}}{3}\,g^{3}_{s}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}\right)^{2}(2\pi)^{2-2\epsilon}\delta^{(2-2\epsilon)}(p_{4}+q_{1}+q_{2})

(231)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\times\,f^{dgx}f^{xfy}f^{yae}\,\mathbf{T}^{a}_{i}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{e,f,g\}}\int[d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{1}][d\hskip-0.80002pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptk_{2}]\,\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}k_{2}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2}+q_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\,\bigg[\frac{(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{1}-k_{2})^{2}}-3\frac{(q_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}}{(q_{1}-k_{2})^{2}}+2\frac{q_{1}^{\mu}}{q_{1}^{2}}\bigg]\varepsilon_{\mu}(p_{4}).

The color structure can be written as follows:

\displaystyle if^{dgx}f^{xfy}f^{yae}\,\mathbf{T}^{a}_{i}\,\mathbf{T}_{j}^{\{e,f,g\}}

(232)

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=\,-\frac{1}{6}\bigg\{\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}\big(+2\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}-\mathbf{T}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{3}+2\mathbf{T}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}-2\mathbf{T}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{5}\big)

) and
(

),

particles, and it is proportional to
